\displaystyle C^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};{\mathbb{C}}\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\nolimits d\oplus\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\nolimits\delta

\displaystyle\ker d

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\nolimits d

\displaystyle\ker\delta

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\nolimits\delta.

In particular,

H^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};{\mathbb{C}}\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)=0.

13.3
Hodge decomposition of
C^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{\pm})\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)

In this section we construct a “Hodge decomposition” of
C^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{\pm})\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)
.

The adjoint representation
\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}\nolimits\colon{\mathfrak{an}}\to\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits({\mathfrak{an}})
extends to an algebra homomorphism
\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}\nolimits\colon U({\mathfrak{an}})\to\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits({\mathfrak{an}})
. Let
I
be the kernel of
\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}\nolimits\colon U({\mathfrak{an}})\to\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits({\mathfrak{an}})
. Consider the algebra

{\mathcal{A}}=C({\mathfrak{an}}\oplus{\mathfrak{an}}^{*})\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits\left(C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{\pm})\right)\otimes U({\mathfrak{an}})/I,

where
C({\mathfrak{an}}\oplus{\mathfrak{an}}^{*})
is the Clifford algebra and
\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits\left(C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{\pm})\right)
is the algebra of all
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear maps from
C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{\pm})
to itself. As before we have a natural representation
{\mathcal{A}}\curvearrowright C^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{\pm})\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)
.

Let
\pi
denote the representation
\mathop{\mathfrak{sl}}\nolimits(2,{\mathbb{R}})\curvearrowright C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{\pm})
. By Proposition
22
there exist a
{\mathbb{C}}
-linear map
\varphi\colon C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{\pm})\to{\mathbb{C}}
and an element
h\in C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{\pm})
such that

\ker\varphi=\pi(E)C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{\pm}),\quad\pi(H)\varphi=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\varphi,\quad\varphi(h)=1.

As we explained in Sections
9.2.1
and
9.3.1
, we can define operators
S,T\colon C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{\pm})\to C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{\pm})
by

\displaystyle S=\pi(E)^{-1}\left(\pi(H)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\right),\quad T=\pi(E)^{-1}\left(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits-\varphi h\right).

Define elements
\hat{d}
,
\hat{\delta}\in{\mathcal{A}}
by

\hat{d}=\hat{d}_{\star}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\otimes 1+\theta_{H}\otimes\pi(H)\otimes 1+\theta_{H}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\otimes H+\theta_{E}\otimes\pi(E)\otimes 1+\theta_{E}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\otimes E

and

\displaystyle\hat{\delta}

\displaystyle=2\hat{d}_{\star}^{\top}\otimes T\otimes E+2H\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\otimes H-2E\otimes S\otimes H

\displaystyle\quad+E\otimes T\otimes\left(1-\sqrt{2}H\right)+\sqrt{2}E\otimes ST\otimes E.

The element
\hat{d}
operates as the differential
d
on the complex
C^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{\pm})\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)
and let
\delta
be the operator determined by
\hat{\delta}
.

Theorem 78
.

We have

\hat{d}\hat{\delta}+\hat{\delta}\hat{d}=1\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\otimes 1-\hat{p}

in
{\mathcal{A}}
, where
\hat{p}
is an element of
{\mathcal{A}}
defined by

\displaystyle\hat{p}

\displaystyle=\left(\theta_{E}\pm\sqrt{2}i\theta_{H}\right)E\otimes\varphi h\otimes\left(1-\sqrt{2}H\pm 2iE\right)

\displaystyle\quad+\theta_{H}\theta_{E}HE\otimes\varphi h\otimes\left(\pm 2iE\right).

Here we identify
{\mathcal{A}}
with

\left(C({\mathfrak{an}}\oplus{\mathfrak{an}}^{*})\otimes{\mathbb{C}}\right)\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\mathop{\mathrm{End}}\nolimits\left(C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{\pm})\right)\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}\left(U({\mathfrak{an}})/I\otimes{\mathbb{C}}\right).

Proof.

The theorem is obtained by a direct computation as in the proof of Theorems
29
and
56
using Proposition
30
, Lemma
74
and the following formulas from Sections
9.2
and
9.3
:

\displaystyle[\pi(H),S]=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}S,\quad[\pi(E),S]=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}

\displaystyle[\pi(H),T]=-\varphi Sh-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}T,\quad[\pi(E),T]=-\varphi h

\displaystyle S\pi(E)=\pi(H),\quad\pi(E)S=\pi(H)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}

\displaystyle T\pi(H)=\pi(E)^{-1}\left(\pi(H)-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\varphi h\right),\quad T\pi(E)=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits

\displaystyle Sh=\pm\sqrt{2}ih.

It may take three to four hours to complete this computation.
∎

Proposition 79
.

We have
\hat{\delta}^{2}=0
in
{\mathcal{A}}
.

Proof.

This follows from an easy computation. We omit a proof.
∎

Let
p\colon C^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{\pm})\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)\to C^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{\pm})\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)
be the operator obtained from
\hat{p}
through the representation of
{\mathcal{A}}
.

Proposition 80
.

We have
\hat{p}^{2}=\hat{p}
in
{\mathcal{A}}
. Hence
p
is a projection.

Proof.

This is proved by an easy computation as well.
∎

Proposition 81
.

The image of the projection
p
is given by

\displaystyle(\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\nolimits p)^{0}

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle(\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\nolimits p)^{1}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{C}}\left(\theta_{E}\pm\sqrt{2}i\theta_{H}\right)\otimes h\otimes\left(H\mp\sqrt{2}iE\right)

\displaystyle(\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\nolimits p)^{2}

\displaystyle={\mathbb{C}}\left(\theta_{H}\wedge\theta_{E}\right)\otimes h\otimes H,

where

(\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\nolimits p)^{i}=\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\nolimits p\cap C^{i}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{\pm})\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)

and we identify
C^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{\pm})\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)
with

\left(\bigwedge^{*}{\mathfrak{an}}^{*}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}\right)\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{\pm})\otimes_{\mathbb{C}}({\mathfrak{an}}\otimes{\mathbb{C}}).

Proof.

By easy computations we get

p\left(1\otimes f\otimes H+1\otimes g\otimes E\right)=0,

\displaystyle p\left(\theta_{H}\otimes f\otimes H+\theta_{H}\otimes g\otimes E+\theta_{E}\otimes k\otimes H+\theta_{E}\otimes l\otimes E\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\theta_{E}\pm\sqrt{2}i\theta_{H}\right)\otimes\varphi(k)h\otimes\left(H\mp\sqrt{2}iE\right)

and

\displaystyle p\left(\left(\theta_{H}\wedge\theta_{E}\right)\otimes f\otimes H+\left(\theta_{H}\wedge\theta_{E}\right)\otimes g\otimes E\right)

\displaystyle=\left(\theta_{H}\wedge\theta_{E}\right)\otimes\varphi(f)h\otimes H,

from which the proposition follows.
∎

Lemma 82
.

We have
dp=0
and
\delta p=0
.

Proof.

Following Proposition
81
, it suffices to show

\displaystyle d\left(\left(\theta_{E}\pm\sqrt{2}i\theta_{H}\right)\otimes h\otimes\left(H\mp\sqrt{2}iE\right)\right)

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\delta\left(\left(\theta_{E}\pm\sqrt{2}i\theta_{H}\right)\otimes h\otimes\left(H\mp\sqrt{2}iE\right)\right)

\displaystyle=0

\displaystyle\delta\left(\left(\theta_{H}\wedge\theta_{E}\right)\otimes h\otimes H\right)

\displaystyle=0.

These equations are proved by direct computations. For the first equation, we use the definition of
\hat{d}
, Equations (
25
) and (
29
). For the second and third ones, we use the definition of
\delta
,
Th=0
and
Sh=\pm\sqrt{2}ih
.
∎

Lemma 83
.

We have
\hat{p}\hat{d}=0
and
\hat{p}\hat{\delta}=0
in
{\mathcal{A}}
.

Proof.

Recall the definitions:

\hat{p}=\left(\theta_{E}\pm\sqrt{2}i\theta_{H}\right)E\otimes\varphi h\otimes\left(1-\sqrt{2}H\pm 2iE\right)+\theta_{H}\theta_{E}HE\otimes\varphi h\otimes\left(\pm 2iE\right),

\hat{d}=\hat{d}_{\star}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\otimes 1+\theta_{H}\otimes\pi(H)\otimes 1+\theta_{H}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\otimes H+\theta_{E}\otimes\pi(E)\otimes 1+\theta_{E}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\otimes E,

\displaystyle\hat{\delta}

\displaystyle=2\hat{d}_{\star}^{\top}\otimes T\otimes E+2H\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\otimes H-2E\otimes S\otimes H

\displaystyle\quad+E\otimes T\otimes\left(1-\sqrt{2}H\right)+\sqrt{2}E\otimes ST\otimes E.

We have
\hat{p}\hat{\delta}=0
since

\displaystyle\left(1-\sqrt{2}H\pm 2iE\right)H=0,\quad\left(1-\sqrt{2}H\pm 2iE\right)E=0

\displaystyle EH=0,\quad E^{2}=0

in
U({\mathfrak{an}})/I\otimes{\mathbb{C}}
and
E^{2}=0
in
C({\mathfrak{an}}\oplus{\mathfrak{an}}^{*})\otimes{\mathbb{C}}
. By these equations and
\varphi\circ\pi(E)=0
, we get

\displaystyle\hat{p}\hat{d}

\displaystyle=\left(\left(\theta_{E}\pm\sqrt{2}i\theta_{H}\right)E\otimes\varphi h\otimes\left(1-\sqrt{2}H\pm 2iE\right)+\theta_{H}\theta_{E}HE\otimes\varphi h\otimes\left(\pm 2iE\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad\left(\hat{d}_{\star}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\otimes 1+\theta_{H}\otimes\pi(H)\otimes 1\right).

By a computation using
\hat{d}_{\star}=-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\theta_{H}\theta_{E}E
and
\varphi\circ\pi(H)=\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}\varphi
, we see
\hat{p}\hat{d}=0
.
∎

Theorem 84
.

We have

\displaystyle C^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{\pm})\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\nolimits p\oplus\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\nolimits d\oplus\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\nolimits\delta

\displaystyle\ker d

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\nolimits p\oplus\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\nolimits d

\displaystyle\ker\delta

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\nolimits p\oplus\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\nolimits\delta

\displaystyle\ker d\cap\ker\delta

\displaystyle=\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\nolimits p

\displaystyle\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\nolimits d\oplus\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\nolimits\delta

\displaystyle=\ker p.

An element
x
of
C^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{\pm})\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)
is decomposed as

x=p(x)+d\delta x+\delta dx

with respect to the first decomposition. The second decomposition implies

H^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{\pm})\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)\simeq\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\nolimits p.

Proof.

This follows from Theorem
78
, Proposition
79
, Proposition
80
, Lemma
82
and Lemma
83
.
∎

13.4
Computation of
H^{*}({\mathcal{F}};{\mathfrak{an}})

In this section we give a “Hodge decomposition” of
C^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(P,{\mathbb{C}})\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)
and determine
H^{*}({\mathcal{F}};{\mathfrak{an}})
as a consequence.

Since
C^{\infty}(P,{\mathbb{R}})\otimes({\mathfrak{an}}\otimes{\mathbb{C}})\simeq C^{\infty}(P,{\mathbb{C}})\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}
as representations of
{\mathfrak{an}}
,

\displaystyle\Gamma\left(\bigwedge^{*}T^{*}{\mathcal{F}}\otimes({\mathfrak{an}}\otimes{\mathbb{C}})\right)

\displaystyle\simeq C^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(P,{\mathbb{R}})\otimes({\mathfrak{an}}\otimes{\mathbb{C}})\right)

\displaystyle\simeq C^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(P,{\mathbb{C}})\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right).

The cohomology of the left hand side is
H^{*}({\mathcal{F}};{\mathfrak{an}}\otimes{\mathbb{C}})
.

By Section
7.3
we have an equivalence

C^{\infty}(P,{\mathbb{C}})\simeq{\mathbb{C}}\oplus g_{\Sigma}\left(C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{+})\oplus C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{-})\right)\oplus{\mathcal{H}}

of representations of
\mathop{\mathfrak{sl}}\nolimits(2,{\mathbb{R}})
, where the Casimir element
\Omega
of
\mathop{\mathfrak{sl}}\nolimits(2,{\mathbb{R}})
acts as a bijection on
{\mathcal{H}}
. The cochain complex decomposes as well:

\displaystyle C^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(P,{\mathbb{C}})\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq C^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};{\mathbb{C}}\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\oplus g_{\Sigma}\left(C^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{+})\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)\oplus C^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{-})\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\quad\oplus C^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};{\mathcal{H}}\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right).

“Hodge decompositions” of
C^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};{\mathbb{C}}\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)
and
C^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{\pm})\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)
are given in Sections
13.2
and
13.3
. Only the second one has a nontrivial cohomology.

To give a “Hodge decomposition” of
C^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};{\mathcal{H}}\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)
, let
\delta
be the operator in Section
13.1
for the complex
C^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};{\mathcal{H}}\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)
. It satisfies

d\delta+\delta d=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\otimes\pi(\Omega)^{2}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits,

where
\pi
denotes the representation
\mathop{\mathfrak{sl}}\nolimits(2,{\mathbb{R}})\curvearrowright{\mathcal{H}}
. We can define

\delta^{\prime}=(\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits\otimes\pi(\Omega)^{-2}\otimes\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits)\delta

since
\pi(\Omega)
is bijective. Then
d\delta^{\prime}+\delta^{\prime}d=\mathop{\mathrm{id}}\nolimits
and
(\delta^{\prime})^{2}=0
. Hence we get the following “Hodge decomposition”:

\displaystyle C^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};{\mathcal{H}}\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)=\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\nolimits d\oplus\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\nolimits\delta^{\prime},

\displaystyle\ker d=\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\nolimits d,\quad\ker\delta^{\prime}=\mathop{\mathrm{im}}\nolimits\delta^{\prime},

\displaystyle H^{*}({\mathfrak{an}};{\mathcal{H}}\otimes{\mathfrak{an}})=0.

Therefore we obtained a “Hodge decomposition” of the entire cochain complex
C^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(P,{\mathbb{C}})\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)
by combining the four decompositions. As a corollary we get a computation of the cohomology.

Corollary 85
.

We have

\displaystyle H^{i}({\mathcal{F}};{\mathfrak{an}}\otimes{\mathbb{C}})\simeq\begin{cases}{\mathbb{C}}^{2g_{\Sigma}}&i=1,2\\
0&\text{otherwise}\end{cases}

and

\displaystyle H^{i}({\mathcal{F}};{\mathfrak{an}})\simeq\begin{cases}{\mathbb{R}}^{2g_{\Sigma}}&i=1,2\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Proof.

The second isomorphism follows from the first since we have

H^{*}({\mathcal{F}};{\mathfrak{an}}\otimes{\mathbb{C}})\simeq H^{*}({\mathcal{F}};{\mathfrak{an}})\otimes{\mathbb{C}}

by (
6
) in Section
2.2
.
∎

14
Computation of
H^{*}\left({\mathcal{F}};{\mathfrak{sl}}(2,{\mathbb{R}})\right)

In this section we compute
H^{*}\left({\mathcal{F}};{\mathfrak{sl}}(2,{\mathbb{R}})\right)
for the adjoint representation
{\mathfrak{an}}\stackrel{{\scriptstyle\mathop{\mathrm{ad}}\nolimits}}{{\curvearrowright}}\mathop{\mathfrak{sl}}\nolimits(2,{\mathbb{R}})
.

By (
6
) in Section
2.2
and Proposition
3
, we have

\displaystyle H^{*}\left({\mathcal{F}};\mathop{\mathfrak{sl}}\nolimits(2,{\mathbb{R}})\right)\otimes{\mathbb{C}}

\displaystyle\simeq H^{*}\left({\mathcal{F}};{\mathfrak{sl}}(2,{\mathbb{R}})\otimes{\mathbb{C}}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq H^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(P,{\mathbb{R}})\otimes\left({\mathfrak{sl}}(2,{\mathbb{R}})\otimes{\mathbb{C}}\right)\right)

\displaystyle\simeq H^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(P,{\mathbb{C}})\otimes{\mathfrak{sl}}(2,{\mathbb{R}})\right).

(34)

From the short exact sequence

0\to{\mathfrak{an}}\to\mathop{\mathfrak{sl}}\nolimits(2,{\mathbb{R}})\to\mathop{\mathfrak{sl}}\nolimits(2,{\mathbb{R}})/{\mathfrak{an}}\to 0

and an isomorphism
\mathop{\mathfrak{sl}}\nolimits(2,{\mathbb{R}})/{\mathfrak{an}}\simeq{\mathbb{R}}_{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}}
of representations of
{\mathfrak{an}}
, we get a long exact sequence

\displaystyle\cdots\to H^{i}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(P,{\mathbb{C}})\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)

\displaystyle\to H^{i}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(P,{\mathbb{C}})\otimes\mathop{\mathfrak{sl}}\nolimits(2,{\mathbb{R}})\right)

\displaystyle\to H^{i}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(P,{\mathbb{C}})\otimes{\mathbb{R}}_{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}}\right)\to\cdots.

Lemma 86
.

The connecting homomorphisms

H^{i}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(P,{\mathbb{C}})\otimes{\mathbb{R}}_{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}}\right)\to H^{i+1}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(P,{\mathbb{C}})\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)

(35)

are zero.

Proof.

Recall that by Theorem
36
and (
26
) in Section
7.3
,

\displaystyle H^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(P,{\mathbb{C}})\otimes{\mathbb{R}}_{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq(3g_{\Sigma}-3)H^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(D_{2}^{+})\otimes{\mathbb{R}}_{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle\quad\oplus(3g_{\Sigma}-3)H^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(D_{2}^{-})\otimes{\mathbb{R}}_{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}}\right)

and by Section
13.4
,

H^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(P,{\mathbb{C}})\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)\simeq g_{\Sigma}H^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{+})\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)\oplus g_{\Sigma}H^{*}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{-})\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right).

By Theorem
20
and an argument in Section
7.3
, we have a decomposition

\displaystyle C^{\infty}(P,{\mathbb{C}})

\displaystyle=g_{\Sigma}C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{+})\oplus g_{\Sigma}C^{\infty}(D_{1}^{-})

\displaystyle\quad\oplus(3g_{\Sigma}-3)C^{\infty}(D_{2}^{+})\oplus(3g_{\Sigma}-3)C^{\infty}(D_{2}^{-})\oplus W

(36)

of representation of
\mathop{\mathfrak{sl}}\nolimits(2,{\mathbb{R}})
, where
W
is a subrepresentation of
C^{\infty}(P,{\mathbb{C}})
. By the definition of the connecting homomorphism, the map (
35
) decomposes with respect to the decompositions of the cohomologies obtained by (
36
). The homomorphisms

H^{i}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(D_{n}^{\pm})\otimes{\mathbb{R}}_{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}}\right)\to H^{i+1}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(D_{n}^{\pm})\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)

for
n=1,2
and

H^{i}\left({\mathfrak{an}};W\otimes{\mathbb{R}}_{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}}\right)\to H^{i+1}\left({\mathfrak{an}};W\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)

are zero, since the domain or the codomain is zero in each case. Hence the map (
35
) is zero as well.
∎

Therefore, by Section
10
and
13
,

\displaystyle H^{i}\left({\mathcal{F}};\mathop{\mathfrak{sl}}\nolimits(2,{\mathbb{R}})\otimes{\mathbb{C}}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq H^{i}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(P,{\mathbb{C}})\otimes{\mathfrak{sl}}(2,{\mathbb{R}})\right)

\displaystyle\simeq H^{i}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(P,{\mathbb{C}})\otimes{\mathfrak{an}}\right)\oplus H^{i}\left({\mathfrak{an}};C^{\infty}(P,{\mathbb{C}})\otimes{\mathbb{R}}_{-\frac{1}{\sqrt{2}}}\right)

\displaystyle\simeq\begin{cases}{\mathbb{C}}^{8g_{\Sigma}-6}&i=1,2\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}

Hence by (
34
),

\displaystyle H^{i}\left({\mathcal{F}};\mathop{\mathfrak{sl}}\nolimits(2,{\mathbb{R}})\right)\simeq\begin{cases}{\mathbb{R}}^{8g_{\Sigma}-6}&i=1,2\\
0&\text{otherwise}.\end{cases}
